Additional animal layouts, existing proposed readings, and a Binjor inscription photo

23 November 2026 · local research continuation

Imported the 2024 animal catalogue and a separate illustrated 33-object register. The additions are published observations about animal imagery, faces and layout. Existing inscriptions and proposed readings are preserved. A Binjor photograph is retained separately with no invented numeric transcription.

What changed from the animal data already in the repo?

The downloaded 2022 workbook exactly matches the archived source file byte for byte. Compared with that baseline, the 2024 workbook adds a sign-coding column populated on 32 named rows and changes common-field label values for 421 shared reference groups. Many changes are nomenclature or corrections, not new archaeological discoveries. The 1,733 linked objects below are existing objects, not the number of newly added specimens. All version differences · Comparison scope and counts.

What the import actually contains

2022 named source rows, plus 353 continuation rows. The named rows include 2002 distinct parseable references and 10 unparsed identifiers. Exact CISI reference matching links the catalogue to 1733 existing objects / 1833 encoded faces. Those are existing artifacts, not newly found inscriptions.

The separate illustrated register contains 33 rows; 28 have existing corpus links. The larger workbook has 31 named rows tagged F3. These are different source inventories. Repeated references, blanks, uncertain animals and PDF/workbook disagreements remain visible. A–F source face labels are not silently equated with the corpus's numerical face IDs.

Three concrete animal/text cases

M-410: A fish in a gharial’s mouth

Existing proposal: 2906.1: Star

The existing single-sign proposal is “Star.” The new source makes the image/text relationship explicit: the fish is in the gharial’s mouth. This can motivate a literal-image versus rebus use comparison, but it does not establish either sound or meaning.

Source animal: Gharial; front-facing sign(s): Mahadevan 59.

M-272: A turning goat looking at two strokes

Existing proposal: 2793.1: Two [818].

The animal turns back toward the two-stroke sign. The source separates that mark from the wheel above the animal. Keep physical layout separate from the stored linear sequence; the animal does not itself establish a numeric or phonetic reading.

Source animal: Turning goat; front-facing sign(s): Mahadevan 99.

M-1719: A box-shaped field element outside the stored text

Existing proposal: 2434.1: Of the High-star-[643] house.

The source labels the rectangle under the unicorn’s nose as Mahadevan 242. The old catalogue already records S621 under its field-object code, while the running inscription is a separate sequence. The addition is independent layout annotation, not a newly discovered sign occurrence or a translation of the box.

Source animal: Unicorn; front-facing sign(s): Mahadevan 242.

Binjor / 4MSR: a site absent by name from the current metadata

An official ASI summary reports eleven excavated seals for the 2015–16/2016–17 seasons. This release recovers one published unicorn-seal photograph with an upper inscription, reported as five characters. The original accession, exact trench and numerical coding are not established here. Eleven seals do not mean eleven inscriptions, and absence of the site name does not prove that no unknown-provenance corpus row depicts the same object.

The photograph comes from a secondary reproduction of the excavation reporting. Its surrounding Vedic and metalwork decipherments are not adopted. Neither a nearby hearth nor the separate study of seven food balls is assigned as this seal's exact context.

Five illustrated candidates missing exact corpus-reference matches

K-37, K-34, B-14, B-11 and M-1718 have no exact CISI-ID match in the current metadata. They were already listed in the older published animal catalogue; they are not newly discovered artifacts. The first four place a single fish or pitchfork-like glyph in an animal scene; this alone does not establish a written word. M-1718 has a multi-sign line plus a frontal glyph, but the source disputes its numeral coding and the PDF/workbook differ. These are image-backed candidates for an explicit corpus crosswalk. Alternate IDs and glyph classification must be resolved before treating them as additional independent inscriptions. All five records.

What this enables next

Use the explicit layouts to distinguish a glyph in an animal scene from the same glyph in the running inscription, and to identify where current occurrence counts depend on that distinction. Recover primary accession-labelled Binjor photos before assigning its signs or scoring a new-site prediction. This import adds no qualifying new family to the frozen 923 elephant/copper test and runs no new semantic validation.
